# Woodstock Rush
## Project Management / TODO / Astra-Codex Handoff

## Mandatory standing workflow

- **PROJECT_TODO.md is the source of truth** for project state, bugs, decisions, backlog and current work.
- **[CODEX_RULES.md](CODEX_RULES.md) contains the mandatory standing workflow/release rules.** Every future Codex task MUST read and follow it before making project changes, even when the individual prompt does not repeat those rules.
- A later explicit instruction from Dan may override a standing rule for that specific task.
- Do not silently modify or weaken CODEX_RULES.md. Changes to standing rules require an explicit instruction from Dan.
- Root AGENTS.md points future Codex tasks to both files.
- From 2026-10-02 the coding agent is Claude Code. The same two files govern it; root CLAUDE.md (created in the 0.67 round) is its discovery pointer.
- **Verification budget (explicit instruction from Dan, 2026-10-05; overrides heavier checking asked for in older rounds and narrows rule 11):** "I do want things fixed to have targeted testing. But things like racing all the tracks twice, I can take care of." "It is more about how many tokens I am spending for things I can do myself."
  - **Do:** one targeted check per change, at the place it changed, showing the thing asked for now works (the hole cannot be fallen into, the bump no longer upsets the bike, the button does its job, a new vehicle loads and sits on its wheels). Compile, launch, and the release steps as always. When a course scene's geometry is touched: the quick "nothing else changed" comparison of that scene's colliders/routes, and one lap of that one course in that one direction.
  - **Do not, unless the round explicitly asks:** race matrices (every course × direction × vehicle), repeat or confirmation runs, forced-shortcut sweeps, world-wide or all-nine-scene sweeps to re-prove a fix, before/after screenshot sets beyond one shot per fix, frame-rate tables on rounds that do not change rendering cost (one worst-view number when they do), reset batteries, or long validation documents.
  - **Dan does:** driving every course and direction, trying every vehicle everywhere, checking that a universal fix holds everywhere, and judging look and feel. He reports through the debug ZIPs.
  - **Controller first (Dan, 2026-10-07):** Dan plays with a controller. Any new or changed menu, screen or prompt must be checked once with a controller only (no mouse, no keyboard) before delivery: every control reachable, focus visible, B goes back.
  - **World changes only where Dan pointed (Dan, 2026-10-08):** fix exactly the place Dan reported. Do not extend a fix to "every similar place" in other scenes or across the world unless the round says so in Dan's words. If a similar problem is seen elsewhere, list it for Dan; do not change it.
  - **Signs (Dan, 2026-10-09):** sign text is part of the sign: one-sided (the back is plain), hidden by terrain and objects like the board, never drawn on top, sized to fit inside its board with a margin, readable at normal sign distance (about 20–30 m), not from across the map. (Mechanism: lettering uses the depth-tested, back-face-culled `Assets/Environment/Phase8/Depth tested world lettering 0.mat`, never a TextMesh's default font material.)
  - **Work in the background (Dan, 2026-10-10):** Dan uses the PC while rounds run; Unity and test players must not take focus or cover his screen. Run the editor in batch mode (`-batchmode -nographics` for scripts that need no rendering; `-batchmode` alone when they render) and never open the Unity editor window unless a step truly needs it. Start test players windowed, not full screen (`-screen-fullscreen 0 -screen-width 1280 -screen-height 720`), minimized and without activation (PowerShell `Start-Process … -WindowStyle Minimized`, or a no-activate launcher), with run-in-background on for check builds so they keep rendering; capture shots and frame sequences to files from the player itself (render texture / screen capture), not by bringing its window forward. Use 3840×2160 full screen only when a check is specifically about 4K full-screen layout, and say so in the results. Close every editor and player you started when the step is done.
  - **Player-facing text is American English (Dan, 2026-10-10).** color, gray, center, meter, favorite, tire, canceled, and so on in everything the player reads (menus, HUD, notices, signs, the launcher, shipped documents). Code names, file names and save / settings keys stay as they are.
  - **Write-up:** the TODO results are a short list: what changed, the one check per item, decisions made, and anything Dan should look at. No separate VALIDATION.md unless a round asks. If a check would take more than a few minutes of play time, skip it and list it under "for Dan to check".

## CURRENT — Leviathan cleanup: every turn clear, wrong roads blocked, gates right, resets onto Leviathan, shortcuts with something on them (Dan's 14 reports) — target 0.117.0-review1 — NOT STARTED

Dan's report `2026-10-14_20-53-07-878_2d1da6` (0.116, Leviathan Forward lap 3 of 4 and Reverse lap 1, Drifter Twin; ZIP in `DebugReports`). Dan (21:17): "A lot to clean up here. It's a little less confusing but there are places that aren't clear whatsoever." **Read all 14 screenshots first.** Everything here applies to the Leviathan races only (0.115 rule: "only change for race, do not change Free Roam or any other races"), world rules as always: no terrain reshaping, nothing floating, signs one-sided and depth-tested.

### Part A — The rule this round enforces
A first-time player must always know where to go, using only what's on screen. So at **every** place where Leviathan's line meets another drivable way (road, trail, driveway, lot, the Mountain Loop's or Street Loop's own routes): **either it's the course (marked: arrow sign before, chevrons on the outside, the next gate visible) or it's blocked** for the race (barricades like Street Loop Reverse's, visible, solid, one-sided signs "ROAD CLOSED"). No third case. Make a **decision-point audit**: a table of every such junction in both directions (position, what's there, marked or blocked, before/after shot). Shortcuts are the only open alternatives and are marked as shortcuts.

### Part D2 — Skipping the mountain must never pay
Dan (21:20): "Also concerned that you could just skip going up the mountain at all and not sure if that will punish you enough. Need to make sure that if someone attempts this, they will suffer a huge time penalty."
0.115 added "Leviathan's lake and mountain gates must be passed in order" (crossing a later gate or the finish doesn't count until they're passed). Prove it and make it unmistakable:
1. Try every way to skip the mountain (and the summit jump) in both directions: turning back early at J, cutting along the lake, any trail, driving over the hill, using a reset. Report each attempt.
2. Skipping must **always cost more than doing it**: the lap doesn't count until the skipped gates are passed (you must drive back), and the screen says so at once, big and clear: **"MOUNTAIN MISSED — GO BACK"**, with the guide pointing to the missed gate. If a lap could somehow end without them, it costs **+60 s per missed mountain gate** (far more than the mountain takes) and the time shows as penalized in the results and is never a record.
3. A reset never moves you past a mountain gate you haven't passed (Part B item 6).
4. Rivals unaffected.
- **Check:** each skip attempt from item 1 driven with the controller, with the time it cost compared to a normal lap's mountain section.

- Free Roam and every other course unchanged (compare as in 0.115).
- Version 0.117.0-review1; usual build and release; close everything started; no background task left running.

## Previous delivery — Leviathan: show where to turn (both directions); Leviathan Reverse: new shortcuts of its own (up to three, Dan keeps the ones he likes) — target 0.116.0-review1 — DELIVERED as 0.116.0-review1 (build 116000), awaiting Dan's review — REVIEWED BY DAN (14 reports; cleanup in 0.117)

### Part A — Leviathan is hard to follow: show where to turn (both directions)
Dan (2026-10-14, 16:46): "These tracks are very messy, there needs to be indicators where to turn. For example on the Forward race you see the checkpoint for where you go after the mountain before you see the CP for the mountain path. And when I tried Reverse, I didn't see where to turn to get off the mountain way."
Leviathan drives some roads twice (the lake shore and stem both ways, the Street Loop around the J), so gates for a later part of the lap are in view before the one you need.
1. **Only the next checkpoint is shown as a gate** (the one after it, faintly, at most). Gates for later parts of the lap are hidden until they're next, so you never see the "after the mountain" gate before the mountain one. Leviathan only.
2. **Turn indicators at every decision point** where the course leaves the road you're on or where two of its own passes meet (the J and the stem, where it leaves the lake for the mountain, where it comes off the mountain in Reverse, Climbing Ridge Cut's entry, the lake joins): chevron boards on the outside of the turn and a big arrow sign before it, visible at racing speed, one-sided and depth-tested (signs rule), grounded, no colliders that catch a car. In the race only.
3. **On-screen help:** the existing waypoint beacon / wrong-way guidance points to the next checkpoint, and the minimap draws the course line ahead (the next ~400 m highlighted) so the next turn shows on the map. Check the existing wrong-way warning fires correctly on Leviathan's twice-driven roads (it must not say "wrong way" on the second pass).
4. **The maps must match the course** (Dan, 16:49: "the map screen didn't match, I don't think, because the Reverse shortcuts on the loop were still marked"). On Leviathan (both directions), the minimap and the big map show **only Leviathan's own route and its own shortcuts**: not the Street Loop Reverse shortcuts (Granite Creek Cut, Laurel Switchbacks), not the scene's Mountain Loop shortcuts or Summit Traverse, not Mountain Loop gates/routes that aren't on Leviathan. Same check for the Tracks preview map and the pause-menu map. Every other course's maps unchanged.
5. Rivals unaffected.
- **Check (built player, controller):** drive both directions with no prior knowledge (follow only what's on screen): driver's-view shots before/after at each decision point, especially Forward's lake/mountain split and Reverse's exit off the mountain; a short clip of each.

### Part B — Leviathan Reverse: new shortcuts

**First, check 0.115's claim (Dan, 16:50: "the shortcuts were still there though").** 0.115 said Granite Creek Cut and Laurel Switchbacks can't be on Leviathan Reverse because their ground exists only in `StreetLoopReverse.unity`, but Dan saw them still there in the Leviathan Reverse race. Find out exactly what is in `MountainLoopReverse.unity` at both shortcuts (ground, route objects, signs, entry markings) with driver's-view shots and a ground probe compared to `StreetLoopReverse.unity`.
- If either shortcut **is drivable** there as it is (no reshaping): put it back on Leviathan Reverse as a proper shortcut (AI line validated, on the maps, timed, never skipping a jump), counted among the "up to three" below.
- If it **isn't drivable** there: nothing of it may look like an open shortcut in the race (hide its entry signs/markings and map line for Leviathan Reverse only, or mark the entry clearly closed, one-sided and depth-tested), so nothing misleads Dan.
- Say which it was, with the evidence.

Dan (2026-10-14, 16:17–16:19), on 0.115's note that Leviathan Reverse has no street shortcuts (Granite Creek Cut and Laurel Switchbacks run on ground reshaped only in `StreetLoopReverse.unity`; in `MountainLoopReverse.unity` that ground differs by up to 19 m): "Why did it remove the shortcuts?" … "Can't there be an option for creating different shortcuts?" So: **new shortcuts made for Leviathan Reverse**, not copies of the old ones.

Rules (Dan's, standing):
- Only on ground and paths that already exist in `MountainLoopReverse.unity` (trails, driveways, lots, the lake shore, open grass, existing roads); **no terrain reshaping, no raised roads, no new roads**. Any world adjustment exists **in the Leviathan Reverse race only** (Dan, 0.115: "only change for race, do not change free roam or any other races"), like 0.115's gully fill: at most small things (a cleared trunk, a fill of a ditch under 1 m).
- Each saves **a few seconds** (about 2–6 s a lap), is riskier, narrower or rougher than the road it cuts, and **never skips a jump** (the summit flight, Downhill Ridge Cut's flights) or the in-order lake and mountain gates (0.115 rule).
- **Something to do on it** where the place allows: a jump that fits the real place (a driveway kicker, a ditch hop, a bank), not a floating ramp.
- **The AI can drive it** (validated line; rivals use it by `ShortcutStrategy` like other shortcuts; no rival resets more than twice in a race).

1. Find candidates along Leviathan Reverse's street part (the Street Loop Reverse stretch in this scene) and, if there is a good one, the lake part. **Build up to three**, each with its entry/exit markings like the other shortcuts, its name (invented, fitting the place), and on the minimap / big map like the others.
2. **Each can be switched on or off** by Dan without a new build (a list in a settings file or a debug menu entry, say which), so he can drive them and keep the ones he likes; all on by default.
3. Leviathan Forward and every other course unchanged.
- **Check (built player, controller):** for each shortcut: a top-down shot, a driver's-view clip through it, the time it saves (player and AI); AI-only Leviathan Reverse races (3 × 2 laps, mixed vehicles): all finish, ≤ 2 resets each; one race driven with the controller.

### Release
Version 0.116.0-review1; usual build and release; close everything started; no background task left running at the end (0.115 left one waiting for an hour after delivery). Delivery notes: the shortcuts side by side and how to switch each off.

## Previous delivery — Half-built new buildings (walls missing from outside); combined course proposals (Street Loop + Mountain) marked in Free Roam — target 0.114.0-review1 — DELIVERED as 0.114.0-review1 (build 114000) — REVIEWED BY DAN ("everything seems fine"; chose Proposal A, built in the Mountain Loop scene; next round below)

### Part A — The new buildings are "half built": walls missing from outside, and they trap you if you drive in (all new buildings)
Dan's report `2026-10-14_04-28-21-329_be9da1` (0.113, Free Roam, Drifter Twin), three spots along Trickum Rd: BUG-001 X=-645.75 Z=350.72 heading 255° "building looks half built"; BUG-002 X=-676.40 Z=369.61 heading 292°; BUG-003 X=-648.69 Z=275.77 heading 257°. Dan (04:49): "I discovered all new buildings are half built, though if you drove in, the walls would form and trap you." Read the screenshots: the walls facing the viewer are not drawn (you see into the building: the back walls' insides, the roof underside), but their colliders are there.
Cause to confirm: the generated building walls (and possibly roofs, cf. 0.104's "flat roofs face up (they were drawn face-down)") have their faces wound inward, so back-face culling hides them from outside and shows them from inside.
1. Fix the generator so **every face of every generated building points outward** (walls, roofs, parapets, canopies, gables, any trim), and rebuild **all** buildings it made: 0.104 (Hwy 92), 0.113 (Trickum Rd / Jamerson / Cherokee Ln) and any other generated ones, in **every scene** that has them. Not by making the material two-sided (that hides the bug and costs performance) unless a thin part truly needs it.
2. An automatic check that runs in the editor: for each generated building, every triangle's normal points away from the building's interior (or each wall is visible in a render from outside on all four sides); list any failure. Run it before release.
3. Colliders unchanged where they match the visible walls; nothing new to catch a car at the road edge.
- **Check (built player, controller):** Dan's three spots, before and after, from his viewpoints; a drive along Trickum Rd and Hwy 92 both ways looking at the buildings; one top-down and one low orbit shot of a strip center and a house.

### Part B — Combined course proposals

Dan (2026-10-12, 10:41): "If I add any more tracks, it could be something a little more like combining tracks we already have. For example a mix of the Street Loop with the mountain tracks. This way the shortcuts on the mountain will save time but not the major part of the race." (The Trickum West new-track idea is dropped.)
Dan (10:48): "Can't we just utilize the instance we have for the mountain races? I think this should connect with the main roads still." Each race scene holds a copy of the whole world, so **prefer building the combined course inside the existing Mountain Loop scene** (its own gates and AI lines on roads that scene already has), not a new scene. First confirm, with a top-down shot, that the Mountain Loop scene's roads connect to the Street Loop roads there; if they don't, say where the gap is.
1. Propose **two combined courses** (A and B) that join existing race roads only (Street Loop roads + a part of the Mountain Loop, linked by roads that already exist in `FreeRoamWorld`): no new roads, no terrain reshaping, no raised roads. Each 3–5 km, a lap of about 2.5–4 minutes; the Mountain section is a minority of the lap so its shortcuts save seconds, not most of the lap. Include the summit jump if the route allows. Say for each: length, the share on Mountain roads, which existing shortcuts are on it and the time each would save, where start/finish fits, and whether the AI can already drive every part (0.112 showed the Mountain Forward shortcuts are not AI-drivable).
2. Mark both in `FreeRoamWorld` with easy-to-see markers Dan can drive: tall posts / arrows every ~25 m, **A bright orange, B bright cyan**, numbered corners, a checkered start, yellow at each shortcut entrance; no collision, no shadows, on the minimap and big map; Settings > "Show track proposals" toggle (default on).
- **Check:** drive both end to end; top-down shots; Part B's files changed = markers and the toggle only.
3. Version 0.114.0-review1; usual build and release. Delivery notes: both proposals side by side, a recommendation, and what building each would involve (a new scene or a course in existing ones, gates, AI lines, campaign place).

- **Part A — half-built buildings.** Cause confirmed: the builder (`Hwy92Roadside`) put each wall on the right-hand side of its footprint edge, which is outside only on a counter-clockwise footprint. All 120 Hwy 92 footprints are counter-clockwise; all 83 Trickum Rd / S Cherokee Ln footprints (0.113) are clockwise. So every Trickum wall, window, door, sign band and parapet faced inward (culled from outside, drawn from inside), and so did each wall collider (one-sided for a car: you could drive in, not out). Fix in the generator, not the material: the footprint's turn (`Hwy92Roadside.Turn`) flips "outward" on a clockwise footprint for the walls, the facade details and the wall collider; the church steeple moves 4.4 m in onto its roof (it stood outside the wall with its solid); the gable ends are drawn once, facing out (they were drawn both ways); the spire faces out. Collider shapes are unchanged (same triangles, now facing out). Built at load in every scene, so this fixes all 9 world scenes; the Hwy 92 buildings come out exactly as before. `SceneryBuildings` (0.78) orients its parts against each building's centre and is not affected.

With the 0.113 builder: Hwy 92 120 parts pass, **Trickum 83 of 83 fail** ([before](Docs/Report114/building-faces-0.113-generator.txt)); fixed: **all 9 scenes, both plans, 0 failures** ([after](Docs/Report114/building-faces-fixed.txt)). | Shortcuts on it (time saved, estimated from lengths at the bot's speed) | Creek Leap (232 m shorter, ~8 s), Fox Gully (100 m, ~3 s), Pine Ridge (172 m, ~6 s), Southwest Cut (24 m, ~1 s), **North Arc: skips the summit climb and jump** (193 m vs 620 m, ~10–15 s) | Granite Creek Cut (266 m, ~9 s), Laurel Switchbacks (459 m, slow climb, ~8–12 s); no Mountain shortcut in Free Roam |
| Start / finish | the Street Loop START FINISH (319, 513), facing south (the existing gate and grid) | the Street Loop Reverse START FINISH (same place), facing north |
| AI today | Street Loop Forward and its four shortcuts: rivals drive them; summit approach and flight: the Mountain Forward rivals fly them every lap (take-off 29.5 m/s); Climbing Ridge Cut and Summit Traverse: AI-validated in 0.113. **New AI work:** the J turns, the lake shore, the joins | Street Loop Reverse, Granite Creek Cut and Laurel (rivals' line): AI-validated. **New AI work:** the J turns, the lake shore and the whole Mountain part (no race line runs the ring this way) |
| Weak spots | J: a hairpin from the stem back onto the Street Loop (corner A12, about 170°) and 65 m of shared two-way stem; a tight bend onto the stem (A11) | J: a hairpin from the Street Loop onto the stem (B8); the same shared stem |

  - **Recommendation: A.** It has the summit jump. The Mountain is a fifth of the lap, and its shortcut (skip the summit) saves seconds, not the lap. Most of it already has validated rival lines (the Street Loop Forward and its shortcuts, the summit flight, Climbing Ridge Cut and Summit Traverse). B has no jump and no Mountain shortcut, and its Mountain part would need new AI lines throughout. Whichever is chosen, race it over **2 laps** (~8 min) rather than 3.
  - **What building it involves (A, in the Mountain Loop scene, as Dan suggested):** one new course road joining the Street Loop Forward road, the lake shore and the Mountain Loop scene's own pieces (CR117 from its start / finish stretch, Climbing Ridge Cut as the main road, CR117 up to CP4 and through the summit flight and landing, back down to the lake). In that scene the Summit Traverse plays the North Arc's part as the shortcut, and the lake's north side differs from Free Roam's (CR117 crosses over it), so the way back down to the lake needs choosing on that scene's own surfaces. Then ~20 gates, AI lines and speed limits for the J hairpin, the stem and the lake (the rest re-anchored from existing lines), and the four Street shortcuts and the Summit Traverse re-anchored as branches of the new road. Campaign place: a Chapter 4 finale or a short Chapter 5. **B** would be built the same way in the Mountain Loop Reverse scene (Street Loop Reverse road reversed in it, Mountain Reverse pieces), with all-new Mountain AI lines.
  - **Markers in FreeRoamWorld** (`TrackProposals.cs`, data `Resources/TrackProposals/TrackProposals.json` from [proposals.py](Tools/Report114/py/proposals.py), [lengths and corners](Docs/Report114/proposals.txt)): a 6.5 m post every 25 m, 7.5 m right of the course (11.5 m on Hwy 92), with an arrow on top pointing the way and a board facing the driver, A bright orange, B bright cyan; a 9 m post with a numbered board (A1–A19, B1–B17, depth-tested one-sided lettering) at each corner; a checkered arch at each start (B's 4 m on); a yellow 8 m post with its arrow into the shortcut at each shortcut entrance. Unlit, no collider, no shadows; 520 posts and 43 boards built in ~20 ms at load, FreeRoamWorld only. Both lines (side by side where they share a road) and the shortcuts in yellow on the minimap and the big map, with a key under the big map. **Settings > Display > "Show track proposals"** (default On, saved as `showTrackProposals`) hides markers and map lines at once. Nothing else in the world changed. Part B files: `TrackProposals.cs`, `TrackProposalsMapOverlay.cs`, the JSON, and one line each in `Scenery.cs` (attach), `RacingMiniMap.cs` / `ExplorationMap.cs` (draw), `RacerSave.cs` / `RaceMenus.Core.cs` (the setting).

## Previous delivery — Rivals stuck on the Laurel Switchbacks shortcut (Wrong Way Round), Retry makes rivals reset, Trickum Rd and the end of S Cherokee Ln nearest Trickum Rd roadside like the real road today, rivals learn the Mountain Forward shortcuts, other vehicles make engine sounds — target 0.113.0-review1 — DELIVERED as 0.113.0-review1 (build 113000), awaiting Dan's review — REVIEW IN PROGRESS (Dan: every new building is half built; 0.114 Part A)

From Code's 0.112 notes (left alone there) and Dan (2026-10-12, 10:41): "I want to scratch the Trickum track, though maybe we could update the road as well as the end of South Cherokee Lane as we did with 92. These are mostly going to be houses and subdivisions throughout here."
Standing rules apply, in particular: world changes only where Dan pointed; built player with the controller; background rule; never Dan's real save; signs one-sided and depth-tested; American English.

### Part A — Rivals stuck on the Laurel Switchbacks shortcut (Street Loop Reverse; Chapter 1 "Wrong Way Round" and everywhere that course is raced)
0.112 measured it: in 9 of 10 AI-only runs one or two rivals (cars, and the Trail Four in Dan's own log) reset 30–140 times or never finish at about (492, 49, −191) (`Docs/Report112/Player/pace-diag-c1-wrong-way-resets.txt`).
1. Find why (the AI line into or out of the shortcut, a vehicle class that can't make the climb or the turn, a collider) and fix it **without changing the course geometry**: correct the AI line, or let a rival take the shortcut only if its vehicle can drive it (otherwise it stays on the main road). The player's shortcut is unchanged.
- **Check:** 10 AI-only runs of Wrong Way Round and 5 of a quick race on Street Loop Reverse with mixed vehicles: no rival resets more than twice in a race, no DNF; report before/after.

### Part B — Retry from the results makes rivals reset and changes their times
1. Retry must start the race exactly like a fresh start from the menu: same grid, rivals placed and settled, no resets at the start, same pace. Find what Retry leaves behind (positions, velocities, AI state, the finish grace) and clear it.
- **Check:** 5 races each finished then retried, on Street Loop Forward and Mountain Loop Forward: rival resets in the first 10 s = 0 on retry, and rival times on retry match a fresh start within normal spread.

### Part C — Trickum Rd and the end of S Cherokee Ln nearest Trickum Rd: roadside like the real road today (as 0.104 did for Hwy 92)
1. **The stretches** (Dan, 10:56): "redoing all of Trickum from 92 all the way until where it meets S Cherokee Lane (which technically it meets Jamerson, but Jamerson is actually represented by like 10 meters in our world, so we are just saying it is part of S Cherokee Lane), and then that end of S Cherokee Lane. It will not be a huge stretch, just a few houses on S Cherokee Ln." So: **all of Trickum Rd, both sides, from Hwy 92 to its junction with S Cherokee Ln / the short Jamerson piece**, plus **the few houses on S Cherokee Ln right at that junction**. Dan's, Kyle's and the named neighbors' houses stay exactly as they are; don't change anything around them. Report the exact extents with a top-down shot. **S Cherokee Ln extent, set by Dan (2026-10-14, 02:14)** with a map screenshot (`SourceArt/Reference/TrickumWest/dan-s-cherokee-section-2026-10-14.png`, his pointer only: placement from OpenStreetMap/NAIP, never from that image): the Trickum Rd / Jamerson Rd junction, the short Jamerson piece, and **Cherokee Ln from that junction east for about 250 m, to just past a large meeting-hall building with a big parking lot on its north side** (the far right of his screenshot). Both sides: mostly houses on wooded lots, that meeting hall with its parking lot north of the lane, and on the corner lot south-west of the Jamerson junction (between Trickum and Jamerson) a church with its parking lot. Use generic buildings and **invented names only** (no real church or organization names or symbols on signs; a plain "church"/"hall" look is fine). Stop there; everything further east on S Cherokee Ln is unchanged.
2. Same method as 0.104 Part B: OpenStreetMap + USGS NAIP for placement only (no Google imagery), aligned on the game's junctions. What's there is **mostly houses and subdivisions** (Dan): generic single-family houses at about the real footprints and setbacks, driveways with curb cuts, mailboxes, subdivision entrances with low brick/stone entrance walls and **invented** subdivision names (one-sided, depth-tested signs), yard trees, fences, a few street lights; any non-residential lot as the reference shows. Sidewalks stop at driveways (0.105 lesson).
3. **Nothing that races uses changes:** no change to the drivable road surface, race lines, gates, AI lines, traffic lanes, shortcuts (all stay reachable at racing speed, including the Street Loop Reverse ones), the jumps (Trickum pavement / trailer jump), the storm drain, the stunt park plot, acorns, hidden-police spots (re-choose the ones on these stretches to suit the new lots), activity sites. Every new thing grounded (rule 4), nothing inside the drivable width + 1 m, no colliders that can catch a car at the road edge.
4. Apply in every scene that shows these stretches (`FreeRoamWorld`, Street Loop Forward / Reverse, Dan's Backyard, others).
5. Performance: worst-view frame time before/after at 3840×2160 and split-screen; no noticeable drop.
- **Check (built player, controller):** driver's-view shots along both stretches both ways, before and after; a top-down shot next to the reference; one lap of each course that uses them with rivals and traffic (no resets caused by the new roadside); the Street Loop Reverse shortcuts and the trailer jump driven at speed.

### Part D — Teach the rivals the two Mountain Loop Forward shortcuts (Chapter 4)
Dan (2026-10-12, 10:48), on Chapter 4 still far too easy: "I am surprised that they don't know how to do this already." 0.112 found the rivals can't drive Mountain Forward's two shortcuts (Climbing Ridge Cut and the other Forward one): they were added for players without validated AI lines; when forced, every rival needed a reset every lap at about road 1650 (`Docs/Report112/Player/pace-x-c4-all-mountain-shortcuts.txt`, `pace-x2-c4-climbing-ridge-cut.txt`). Dan's Summit Final first lap was about 40 s against the rivals' 108 s.
1. Give both shortcuts proper AI lines (entry, speed limits, the run-up and landing if any, the exit merge back onto the road), **without changing the course geometry**. Validate them like the Reverse course's Summit Traverse (0.112: taken every lap by every rival, 0 resets).
2. In Chapter 4 campaign races and quick races on Mountain Loop Forward, rivals then use them (by `ShortcutStrategy`, as on the other courses).
3. Re-measure Chapter 4 with 0.112's method (AI field alone, 5 runs, against Dan's logged first-run times) and, with the 0.112 knobs only (difficulty, rival vehicles, the per-event pace ≤ 1.15), land the races in 0.112's windows (5–15 s; Summit Final 0–8 s) or stop at the closest and say why.
- **Check:** 10 AI-only runs on Mountain Loop Forward with mixed vehicles: rivals take both shortcuts, no rival resets more than twice in a race, no DNF; before/after Chapter 4 margin table; one Chapter 4 race driven with the controller.

### Part E — Other vehicles make engine sounds
Dan (2026-10-13, 19:53–19:55): "So do other cars not make sounds?" … "well yes." Today only the player's vehicle has engine sound (`VehicleAudio`, synthesized clips, `spatialBlend = 0`): `RaceDirector` destroys `VehicleAudio` on every rival clone, traffic has none, and `GetawayChase` adds a 2D `VehicleAudio` to cop cars.
1. **Rivals, traffic and police cars** get engine sound that comes from where they are in the world (3D, distance falloff, no doppler artifacts): you hear a rival closing in behind or beside you and fading as you pull away. Pitch and load from that car's own speed and throttle (reuse `VehicleSoundSynthesis` / `VehicleAudio`'s engine model, not the player's input). Tire and impact sounds for them only if cheap and not noisy.
2. **Only the nearest few** (about 4 rivals/cops + 2 traffic) play at once, with smooth fade in/out when the set changes; the rest are silent. Other cars sit somewhat quieter than the player's engine at the same distance; the player's own engine stays exactly as now (2D).
3. Police cars: engine under the siren (siren/lights unchanged). Mower, motorcycles and ATVs sound like themselves, not like cars.
4. Split-screen: each player hears the cars around their own vehicle (one listener between the two, or the nearest-car set per player; choose what sounds clear and say how). Menus, pause and results: other cars silent like the player's engine.
5. Follows the existing Vehicle volume setting. No frame-rate drop at 3840×2160 or in split-screen (report before/after).
- **Check (built player, controller):** a race with rivals (a rival passing on each side), Free Roam traffic, a Getaway at heat 3+, a split-screen race: a short screen-and-audio recording of each, and a note of what you hear. Nothing else about the player's sound changed.

## QUEUED NEXT — Stunt Races (stunt vehicles only, Mountain courses, own records) and a Stunt chapter in the campaign — target 0.118.0-review1 — NOT STARTED (do not start until Claude (chat) promotes this to CURRENT, after 0.117)

Dan (2026-10-14, 19:24–19:26): "With all the air you get on the mountain jumps, it would be a fun place to do stunts" … "or we can have special stunt-only mountain races with their own separate records and it would mix well with a stunt campaign." Stunt vehicles stay out of normal races, the existing campaign chapters and their records (fairness, balancing).

### Part A — Stunt Races
1. A race type **Stunt Race**: stunt vehicles only (stunt buggy, stunt motorcycle, and any later stunt vehicle) for the player and every rival; air steering on; the stunt park's landing rules (0.107: 1.5 s recovery window, bail only on a real roof/side landing).
2. Courses: **Mountain Loop Forward / Reverse and Leviathan Forward / Reverse** (proposed; Dan to confirm). Same routes, gates, shortcuts and in-order rules as the normal races.
3. **Scoring (proposed default): position by finish time, with a trick bonus**: each landed trick or combo knocks time off your race time (e.g. 0.5 s per 500 trick points, capped so a race can't be won by tricks alone), shown live ("−2.5 s TRICK BONUS"). **Rivals do tricks too** (Dan, 19:28: "the rivals can't do stunts?"): at the big jumps whose flights are known (the summit flights, Downhill Ridge Cut's flight, the Street Loop jumps on Leviathan; the authored flight data gives airtime and landing), a rival picks a trick its airtime allows (flip, roll, 360, or a combo on long flights) by its skill and difficulty, rotates through it with the same air steering the player has, and lands it, with an occasional bail (more on Easy, rare on Hard) that costs it the bonus and a little time, never a stuck reset. Rivals earn the same trick bonus. Their tricks show briefly over them ("BACKFLIP") when near the player. Tune so you have to trick to win.
4. **Own records**: separate Records / Top 10 boards for Stunt Races (race time with bonus, best lap, best combo per course); never mixed with the normal boards. Ghosts per Stunt Race course.
5. Race > Tracks: a "Stunt Race" option (or mode toggle) that only appears once a stunt vehicle is owned. Split-screen supported.
6. Trick pop-ups smaller in races than in the park, never covering the position/lap HUD.

### Part B — Stunt chapter in the campaign
1. A separate **Stunt chapter** (after Chapter 4 or opened by winning the first stunt vehicle; proposed: opens with the Stunt Buggy), its own events, rivals on stunt vehicles, its own prize at the end (proposed: a stunt-vehicle Cup edition, like 0.111's).
2. Events (proposed, 6–7): a Stunt Race on Mountain Loop Forward; a trick-score challenge at the summit jump (best combo in 3 tries); the 10-minute Stunt event at the park (a medal target); a Stunt Race on Mountain Loop Reverse; a "land it clean" event (fly the summit jump N times without a bail); a Leviathan Stunt Race; the final, a 2-lap Stunt Race on Leviathan Forward.
3. **Split score for Stunt chapter races** (Dan, 19:32: "you get 10 points for finishing first place and 10 points for scoring the most stunt points"): each race ranks everyone twice, by **finish** (race time, no trick time bonus here) and by **stunt points** (all landed tricks and combos in the race). Each ranking pays 10 / 7 / 5 / 3 / 2 / 1 points by place (the championship scale). The event result is the **total** (max 20); ties go to the better finish. Passing = top 3 by total, winning = 1st, as other campaign races. The results screen shows both columns and the total; the HUD shows your live finish place and stunt-point place.
   Quick Stunt Races (Part A) keep the trick time bonus by default, with **Split score** offered as a setting there too.
4. Its own balancing via the results log (tagged `stunt`), same method as 0.112.
- **Check (built player, controller):** one Stunt Race each course; a trick bonus applied; records on their own boards; the chapter's first two events and the final from a test save; normal races, records and campaign unchanged.

## Previous delivery — Vehicle stats on every campaign garage screen; Fence Line Smash counts what you smash; fresh-run prize congratulations; Cup edition vehicles for the championships — target 0.111.0-review1 — DELIVERED as 0.111.0-review1 (build 111000), awaiting Dan's review — REVIEW PENDING (Dan finished the fresh run on 0.110 before installing 0.111)

Dan (2026-10-11, 20:22), playing 0.110: "On the campaign garage you should see the car stats." Then (20:36): "There is space beside the car to put stats without needing to go to the garage." So the main fix is the **event setup screen**: the vehicle preview card ("Your vehicle: …", with the prize card) has empty space beside it. **Put the five stat bars there, beside the card**, updating at once as Dan steps the vehicle left/right on the Vehicle row, so he can compare without opening the garage. Same on the championship setup. The other screens in item 1 are the follow-on check.

The five stat bars (Top speed, Acceleration, Grip, Handling, Weight / contact; `RaceMenus.GarageStats.cs`, 0.82 Part D, upgrade marks since 0.90) show on the main garage, the campaign vehicle pick (`RaceMenus.Pick.cs`) and the Shop (`RaceMenus.Shop.cs`), but Dan didn't see them on the campaign garage he used.
### Part A — The stat bars wherever the campaign shows a vehicle

1. Find every campaign screen where a vehicle is shown or chosen, in **both** campaign slots (MY CAMPAIGN and FRESH RUN, 0.110): the event setup's vehicle row and preview card, championship setup, the vehicle pick ("A: garage"), the Shop (owned and for sale), the upgrades page, the prize preview. List which showed the bars and which didn't, and which one Dan most likely meant.
2. Every one of them shows the same five bars for the vehicle in view, with **that campaign's upgrades** drawn on top (as the Shop does for owned vehicles), for locked or for-sale vehicles the stock bars. Same scale as the main garage, so numbers compare across screens.
3. Never over the preview; no change to navigation (display only); fits at 3840×2160 and 1280×720; vehicle pictures never squished.
- **Check (built player, controller, test copy of Dan's save):** shots of each campaign screen in both slots with the bars; one upgraded vehicle showing its upgrade marks.

### Part B — Fence Line Smash doesn't count what Dan smashes
Dan (2026-10-11, 20:51), Chapter 3 "Fence Line Smash" (`c3-fence-smash`, Dan's Backyard Forward, site `smash-01`, 45 s, targets 3 / 6 / 10, moto, fresh run): "Don't understand Fence Line Smash. First attempt smashed a bunch, said I smashed nothing. The second time it said I smashed 9 but I kept smashing more and nothing was working."
His results log: attempt 1, 1 reset, recorded 0 / no medal; attempt 2, no reset, 9 / silver.

What the code does today (`ArcadeActivities.Smash`, `FinishSmash`, `Recovered`; `CampaignRun` smash branch):
- any reset/recovery calls `Cancel()`: the attempt stops and the count is cleared, but the event keeps running, so everything smashed after it is ignored, and at the time limit `CampaignRun` records **0, "nothing smashed"** instead of the live count (attempt 1);
- a smash only counts when `warm >= .5` (the vehicle grounded and upright for half a second, a jump rule), so props hit while bouncing or tipped by the previous hit are ignored;
- only props in the site's `props` list count; other breakables in the same fence line don't.
1. **Every breakable prop in the smash area counts once**, whatever the vehicle is doing (airborne, tipped, sliding). Drop the `warm` gate for smashes (keep it for jumps). If some breakables in the area are deliberately not part of it, make them non-breakable or clearly outside; Dan should never smash something there that doesn't count.
2. **A reset doesn't cancel a smash event**: count and clock keep going; you carry on smashing.
3. **The time limit records the live count** (medal from it), never 0 when something was smashed.
4. The event ends at the time limit, or at once when every prop in the area is down (not when gold is reached: keep counting past gold). Say what the area holds (e.g. "14 to smash").
5. **Make it understandable:** the intro says "Smash as many fence panels, mailboxes and bins as you can in 45 s: 3 bronze, 6 silver, 10 gold"; the HUD shows the count, the time left and the next medal (like the Stunt event's goal line); each smash pops "+1 (7)". The props that count are visible as such (no hidden ones).
6. Same fixes for Free Roam's smash activity sites.
- **Check (built player, controller, test copy of the fresh-run save):** smash with the moto and a car: every hit counts, including while airborne; reset mid-event: the count stays and smashing continues; let time run out with 8: records 8 / silver; smash everything before time: ends at once with the full count.

### Part C — The fresh run's chapter prize gets its congratulations
Dan (2026-10-11, 21:08), fresh run, after winning the Backyard Final at 21:06: "No reward vehicle for completing Chapter 3?"
He did win it: the log shows "Vehicle: Ridge Scrambler" unlocked and the fresh run's garage now holds the scrambler. But `Campaign.Apply` raises the 0.108 congratulations only `if (... && !Fresh)`, so in the fresh run nothing told him.
1. In the fresh run, a chapter prize (and anything else the run unlocks: a championship, a chapter) shows the same **"CONGRATULATIONS! You unlocked the <vehicle>"** message with its picture as a new player would see, after the results screen, until dismissed. It is the fresh run's unlock: nothing outside the fresh run changes (Dan already owns it in his game), and it doesn't re-trigger in MY CAMPAIGN.
2. The results screen of a final shows the prize revealed ("PRIZE WON: Ridge Scrambler") in both slots.
3. Check every other `Fresh` suppression added in 0.110 for the same mistake (anything a new player would see that the fresh run hides) and list them.
- **Check (test copy of Dan's fresh-run save at the Chapter 3 final):** win it: results show the prize; the congratulations follows; the scrambler appears in the fresh run's garage; MY CAMPAIGN unchanged.

### Part D — A "Cup edition" vehicle for winning each championship
Dan (2026-10-11, 21:40–21:44): "No vehicles on the championships?" Today the four championships (`CampaignData.Cups`: Street, Woodland, Backyard, Woodstock Grand) pay money only (+ the Grand's champion paint); only chapter finals award vehicles. Of two options Claude offered, Dan chose **new championship-only versions of existing vehicles** (nothing leaves the Shop).
1. **Four Cup editions**, one per championship, each based on an existing vehicle that fits that point of the campaign (a step up from what a player typically has after that chapter; the Grand's on one of the best). Named "<Vehicle> Cup" (the Grand's "<Vehicle> Champion"). Say which bases you chose and why.
2. Each has its **own livery** (the cup's colors with gold trim and a small cup emblem; original art, no real brands) and a **small stat boost over its base, about +5% across the five stats**, shown in the stat bars. They can be upgraded in the campaign like the others; in Race / Free Roam they are stock like every vehicle.
3. **Won by winning the championship** (1st overall), not by finishing it: same as the chapter-final prizes. The championship setup shows the prize card (a silhouette until won), like a chapter final.
4. Winning one shows the **"CONGRATULATIONS! You unlocked the <name>"** message (0.108) after the results, in both campaign slots; in the fresh run it belongs to the fresh run only (Part C). Won in MY CAMPAIGN, it is also usable in Race, Free Roam, split-screen and the other modes, like other unlocks.
5. **Both slots, retroactively:** in MY CAMPAIGN and in the FRESH RUN, a championship already won before this build grants its Cup edition on load, with the one-time note and the congratulations (as 0.92 did for chapter prizes). Dan (21:47) is playing the fresh run's championships before 0.111 lands.
6. Models: reuse the base model with the livery; vehicle pictures never squished; the garage, Shop, pick and stat bars handle them.
- **Check (test copies of Dan's saves):** win each championship once (test shortcuts for the setup allowed, say so): prize card, results, congratulations, garage entry with livery and boosted bars; MY CAMPAIGN gets the ones already won on load; the fresh run's doesn't leak to MY CAMPAIGN.

### Part A — Split-screen Top 10 rank shown twice as the same number

### Balancing groundwork (was queued as 0.110)

Dan (2026-10-11, 10:02): folded into 0.109 because it is small. Dan (10:01) agreed to Claude's balancing plan: (1) the game logs every result while Dan plays normally; (2) Dan plays the campaign a session or two, adding a note in a report when something feels too easy or too hard; (3) Claude reads the log and proposes concrete tuning (rival pace per chapter/difficulty, medal targets, unlock goals) for Dan to approve; (4) Code applies it in a later round; repeat once or twice. **Parts C and D only add the log and the balance sheet. Change no tuning value.**

### Part C — Results log (written automatically, nothing for Dan to do)
1. Append one JSON line per finished **or abandoned** attempt to `results-log.jsonl` in the game's data folder (`C:\Users\danmo\AppData\LocalLow\DefaultCompany\Racer\BalanceLog\`), kept across sessions (roll to a new file past ~5 MB, never delete). Also copy it into every exported debug-report ZIP.
2. Covered: campaign races and chapter events (with chapter and event id, practice laps separately marked), quick races, split-screen races, Free Roam activities (jumps, speed traps, ghost), Getaway, Speed Patrol, the Stunt event.
3. Each line: time stamp, version, mode, course, direction, laps, difficulty, weather/time of day, player vehicle; **result** (place, total time, best lap, finished/abandoned and how far in); **each rival**: name, vehicle, finish time or gap to the player at the end, and any AI catch-up/rubber-band state the game uses; **medal** earned, all medal targets, and the margin to the next one; resets, wipe-outs and big collisions; attempt number for that event (how many tries it took) and whether it was a retry. Getaway: duration, max heat, cops at peak, escaped/busted. Speed Patrol: speeders caught/escaped. Stunt event: score, best combo, medal. Any vehicle unlock earned in that attempt.
4. Split-screen: one line per player. Never write Dan's real save from tests; test runs write to a test log path.
5. Cheap: no frame-time cost; written once at the end of each attempt.

### Part D — Balance sheet (current values, read-only)
1. A generated `BalanceLog\balance-sheet.md` (rewritten on each game start) listing every current tuning value in plain tables: rival pace / skill per campaign chapter and per difficulty, AI catch-up settings, medal targets for every event (races, activities, Getaway, Speed Patrol, Stunt event), practice-lap rules, unlock goals for every vehicle, police chase pacing per heat level, and where each value lives in code. This is what Claude reads to propose changes.

### Part E — Checks and release
1. Built player, controller, background rule: one campaign race finished, one quit halfway, one activity, one Getaway, one Stunt event with the test limit, one split-screen race: show the log lines; show the balance sheet. Test log path only.

### Part A — A live combo keeps the event going past 0:00
Dan: "It shouldn't end if you have a combo going and there is still seconds left. You should be able to continue going until either you crash or you didn't make it to another trick in time for the 5 second count down."
1. This replaces 0.107 Part E's "open combo banked at once". When the clock reaches 0:00 and a player has a **combo going** (in the air, in the loop, or on the ground with the 5 s chain countdown still running), the event **continues for that player** ("COMBO OVERTIME" with the chain countdown big on screen). They keep chaining tricks as long as they keep making the next trick inside 5 s.
2. It ends for that player when the combo ends: a bail/crash, or the 5 s countdown runs out on the ground. The combo is banked (points × links, as now) and then the results show. A bail ends it the same way it does before 0:00 (0.107 rules: the combo ends, its banked points as the game already handles a bail).
3. A player with **no combo going** at 0:00 (and not in the air) is done at 0:00. Someone in the air at 0:00 with no combo yet: that flight finishes (0.107), and if it lands a trick it starts a combo that can continue under this part.
4. Split-screen: each player continues independently; the results wait until both are done; the one who is done sees "Waiting for P2" (their final score shown).
5. No new time limit needed: the 5 s rule ends it. Keep only a hang-safety (e.g. the vehicle stopped for 5 s ends it).
- **Check:** at about 0:03 start a chain and keep chaining past 0:00 for at least three more tricks: all count; then miss the 5 s: banked, results. Once with a bail in overtime. Once in split-screen with one player in overtime.

### Part B — Show the goal during the event
Dan: "The event should show your goal (or how far you are away from it) during the race."
1. On the event HUD, always show the **next medal**: its colored medal icon, its target, and **points still needed** (e.g. "SILVER 25,000 — 6,420 to go"). When it's reached, a short "SILVER!" moment and the line switches to the next medal; after gold, show "GOLD ✓" and your margin over it.
2. Split-screen: each player's own line in their view. Same style as the other events' medal display; nothing overlapping the trick/combo text or the minimap at 3840×2160 and 1280×720.
- **Check:** shots at the start, between bronze and silver, and after gold, solo and split-screen.

### Part C — A congratulations every time you unlock a vehicle
Dan: "When you unlock the bike there should have been a 'Congratulations, you have unlocked the bike' message. It should do this anytime you unlock a vehicle."

You unlocked the <vehicle name>"**, with the vehicle's picture (never squished) and how you earned it in one line, until Dan dismisses it with A / Enter (not a timer). If several unlock together, one after another.
3. Saved so each shows exactly once, and never lost: if the game closes before it's shown, it shows next time.

## Previous delivery — Stunt park: forgiving landings, tricks that leave the park count, reset back into the park, 5 s to chain, finish the trick at the buzzer, no hay bales in the way — target 0.107.0-review1 — DELIVERED as 0.107.0-review1 (build 107000), awaiting Dan's review — REVIEWED BY DAN (four last items to mark the stunt course complete, in 0.108)

Dan (2026-10-11, 04:25–04:32), after playing 0.106: "Stunt course generally fun, but can use some improvements." His six points are quoted in each part. Change only what these parts say; the park layout, vehicles, air steering, scoring values and medals stay as 0.106 unless a part says otherwise. Code: `Assets/Scripts/ArcadeActivities.Stunts.cs`, `StuntEvent.cs`, `StuntPark*.cs`, `VehicleRespawn.cs`.

### Part A — Landings far too unforgiving
Dan: "It is far too unforgiving. Rush would give you the opportunity to recover, but here even if I land on my back wheels or front wheels, it is claiming I land on the roof — often I hadn't come remotely close to landing on the roof."

Cause seen in the code: `StuntContact` bails the instant any part of the body touches ground while `car.transform.up.y < .3` (tilted about 73° or more), and calls it "roof" as soon as `up.y < 0`. A steep landing on the back or front wheels (an under- or over-rotated flip) trips it, though the car would come down on its wheels.
1. **No instant bail on contact.** When the vehicle touches down at any angle, give it a **recovery window (about 1.5 s)**: if it comes down onto its wheels and drives on (all wheels grounded for 0.3 s, the existing `sStable` test) within that window, the landing **counts**, with the tricks rotated so far (rounded as now). Rush-style: nose-first or tail-first slams that bounce back onto the wheels are landings.
2. **A bail only when it really happened:** the vehicle is still on its roof or side when the window ends, or the roof itself (contact point on the top of the body, vehicle more than about 100° over) hit the ground hard, or the existing crash/wipe-out (`VehicleConfiguration.WipedOut`) fired. Check what triggers `WipedOut` on these landings for both stunt vehicles and the race cars: a hard landing on the wheels must not count as a wipe-out.
3. **The message names what actually happened:** "Landed on the roof" only when the roof hit; "Landed on the side" only when the vehicle ended on its side; otherwise no bail message.
4. Optional, if it feels right: a landing that needed the recovery window shows "SKETCHY LANDING" and still scores in full. No point penalty.
- **Check (built player, controller):** with the stunt buggy, the stunt motorcycle and Dan's usual race car: land a backflip under-rotated onto the back wheels, one over-rotated onto the front wheels, and one 90° sideways onto two wheels that falls back: all **count**. A real roof landing and a real side landing: both **bail**, with the right message. Report each with the vehicle's tilt at first contact.

### Part B — A trick that takes you out of the park still counts
Dan: "If a trick takes you out of the park, I feel like it should still count."
1. A flight that **takes off inside the park** counts wherever it lands (in the Stunt event, in Free Roam best combo, in split-screen). Remove "Outside the park: not counted" for those. A flight that takes off outside the park (e.g. a Hwy 92 bump) still doesn't count in the event.
- **Check:** launch off the edge kicker so the landing is outside the fence: points added in the event.

### Part C — Reset during the event puts you back in the park
Dan: "If you hit reset while you are in the event but you had landed outside of the stunt park, it should reset you in the stunt park and not on the road."
1. During the Stunt event (solo and each split-screen player), **any reset** puts you back inside the park, facing into it, at a clear spot (the last safe spot you were on in the park, or the park start if none), never on Hwy 92. In Free Roam outside the event, resets work as today.
- **Check:** in the event, land outside the park on the Hwy 92 side and press reset: you're in the park. Same in split-screen for player 2.

### Part D — 5 seconds to chain a combo
Dan: "It is almost impossible to get to another stunt within 2 seconds. Should be at least 5 seconds."
1. `ComboGap` 2 s → **5 s** on the ground between tricks. Keep the countdown shown on screen ("next within … s"). Report whether the park's flow lets you reach a next feature in 5 s from each landing; if a landing is too far from anything, say which (don't rebuild the park this round).

### Part E — Finish the trick at the buzzer
Dan: "If you are in the middle of a stunt and the time runs out, you should be able to complete the stunt."
1. When the event clock reaches zero while a player is **in the air or in the loop**, the clock shows 0:00 ("FINISH THE TRICK!") and the event waits until that flight lands (or bails). The landing scores as usual, then any open combo is **banked at once** (no 5 s wait), then the results show. Grounded players at 0:00: open combo banked at once, results show.
2. Split-screen: wait for both players' flights.
3. A safety limit (about 10 s) so the event can't hang.
- **Check:** take off from the biggest kicker about 1 s before 0:00 with a combo open: the trick and combo count in the final score.

### Part F — No hay bales in the way
Dan: "I don't understand the purpose of the rolls of hay, they just get in the way."
1. Remove every hay bale from the riding area (`HayRow` calls in the park build). Where a row marked a run-up or a landing edge, nothing replaces it. If any are worth keeping as decoration, only along the outside of the perimeter fence, out of any line you can ride. List what was removed.

### Part G — Checks and release
1. All checks in the built player with the controller, background rule; test saves only, never Dan's real save.
2. One full Stunt event solo (use the test limit if available to shorten it, and say so) showing A–F together, and one in split-screen.
3. Version 0.107.0-review1; usual build and release; close everything started. Delivery notes per part with evidence and what Dan should try first.

## Previous delivery — Stunt park on the Hwy 92 lot (Rush 2-style), stunt buggy and stunt motorcycle with air steering — target 0.106.0-review1 — DELIVERED as 0.106.0-review1 (build 106000), awaiting Dan's review — REVIEWED BY DAN ("generally fun"; six improvements in 0.107)

Dan (2026-10-10, 20:11–20:15): "I was thinking something similar to the stunt course for Rush USA" (the stunt arena in *Rush 2: Extreme Racing USA*, N64: a walled area with ramps of several sizes, half-pipes, a loop and two levels, points for air and flips/rolls landed on the wheels). Claude proposed the points below; Dan: "this all sounds perfect. Let's try this. Maybe we can have special vehicles for the steering in air part."

**Inspired by, not copied:** no names, layout, art, logos or sounds from Rush 2 or any other game. Our own place, our own look.

### Part A — The stunt park itself
1. **Where:** the open plot kept free in 0.104 Part B item 7 (the real subdivision's footprint across Hwy 92 from S Cherokee Ln). Stay inside it. Entrance: a driveway off Hwy 92 with a one-sided, depth-tested sign with an invented name (signs rule). Grounded, level base. Hwy 92's road surface, race lines, gates, AI and traffic lanes do not change.
2. **Look:** a local dirt-lot stunt park, not a futuristic arena: packed-dirt floor, plywood and dirt ramps, hay bales, shipping containers, an old school bus and a row of junk cars to jump over, a low perimeter (fence / tire wall) so you can't fly onto Hwy 92 by accident.
3. **Features (all with sloped, downhill landings sized to their launch: big jumps must land on a down-slope, never flat):**
   - kickers from small to huge, in a sensible flow so you can chain them;
   - a gap jump over the school bus, and one over the junk-car row;
   - quarter-pipes / a half-pipe along the edges;
   - a two-level deck you launch up onto and drop off;
   - **one loop** and **one barrel-roll ramp** (corkscrew kicker) as showpieces: the loop drivable at a speed the stunt vehicles reach easily; normal vehicles may fail it.
4. Whatever is on the ramps (seams, ramp-to-ground joints) is one smooth surface: no catches like the Cabin run-up had (0.101–0.103 lessons).
5. Appears in `FreeRoamWorld` and in every race scene that shows that plot (as scenery there; usable only in Free Roam and the Stunt event).
6. **Performance:** report the worst-view frame time before and after at 3840×2160 and in split-screen; no noticeable drop.

### Part B — Stunt scoring (built on the existing jump scoring in `ArcadeActivities`)
1. Keep the existing jump points (distance, air time, clean landing). Add tricks: **flip** (forward/back, each full 360°), **roll** (sideways 360°), **spin** (flat 360° turns), plus loop and barrel-roll ramp completions. Show each like the current "CLEAN JUMP / … pts" line, e.g. "BACKFLIP + 360 SPIN / 412 pts".
2. **Landing:** points count only if you land on your wheels; landing on the roof or side loses that jump's tricks (show "BAILED").
3. **Combo:** tricks chained with less than about 2 s on the ground between them multiply (show the multiplier); a bail ends the combo.
4. Works for every vehicle; flips and rolls are just much easier in stunt vehicles (Part C).

### Part C — Stunt vehicles with air steering (buggy and motorcycle)
1. Two new **stunt vehicles**, our own designs: a light, high-suspension **stunt buggy** and a **stunt motorcycle** (dirt-bike style, built on the existing motorcycle handling). Dan (20:23): "I would like a motorcycle either in addition or instead of a truck" — so the bike replaces the truck in this round; no truck. Generic look, invented names, no real brands. The rider stays on the bike through flips and rolls when landed on the wheels; a bail drops the rider like any other crash in the game.
2. **Air steering only in stunt vehicles:** while all wheels are off the ground, the left stick (and keyboard equivalents) tips the vehicle forward/back and rolls it sideways, and the right stick or bumpers spin it flat. Strong enough to do a full flip on the big kickers, gentle enough to level out a landing. All other vehicles behave exactly as today, everywhere.
3. **Availability:** usable in Free Roam (anywhere, not only the park) and the Stunt event. **Not** in races, campaign races, Getaway or Speed Patrol. The buggy is available from the start; the stunt motorcycle unlocks with the first Gold medal in the Stunt event (unlock notice like the others; goal shown in the garage).
4. Vehicle pictures in menus never squished (standing rule).

### Part D — Two ways to play
1. **Drop-in (Free Roam):** drive in and score freely; best single combo saved.
2. **Stunt event:** start it from the park entrance (like the other Free Roam activities) and from the menu: **10 minutes** to score as many points as possible. Bronze / silver / gold as colored medals with their point values shown before you start (same style as the other events). Top 10 for the Stunt event in Records (split-screen included, names as the other Top 10s).
3. **Split-screen:** both players in the park at once, both scored, a winner at the end with the usual celebration.
4. **Acorns:** a few new acorns on the hardest-to-reach spots (the top deck, on top of the bus, the top of the loop's platform if there is one), added to the acorn count and map areas as before.
5. Map: the park shown on the minimap and the big map with its name.

### Part E — Checks (built player, controller first, background rule)
1. Driver's-view shots of the park from the Hwy 92 entrance, and a top-down shot with the plot outline from 0.104.
2. With each stunt vehicle: every feature driven at least once, including the loop and barrel roll; a backflip, a roll and a 360 spin landed and scored; a bail shown; a combo with multiplier. With Dan's usual race car: the kickers and gap jumps (no air steering, as today).
3. A full 10-minute Stunt event solo and in split-screen (Dan, 20:23: "lets go with 10 minutes"); a pause and resume in the middle keeps the clock and score; medals and Top 10 written to a **test save, never Dan's real save**.
4. A Street Loop race lap each way past the park: nothing changed on the road; Hwy 92 traffic unaffected.
5. Report scores reached for bronze/silver/gold tuning (gold should take real effort).

### Part F — Build, release
Version 0.106.0-review1; usual build and release steps; close everything started. Delivery notes per part with evidence and what Dan should try first.

A gravel **drive** from Hwy 92's north edge at x 120 (from the road's own height there; nothing is built over the road; the step at the edge is 0.6 cm) rises 2 m over 51 m to the floor; the 0.104 sidewalk and one roadside tree stop at the drive. Laid out as one counter-clockwise circuit (kicker line → car gap → deck, drop, loop → half-pipe) plus three lines across the middle (huge table, barrel roll, bus gap).
  - **Making the loop work:** wheels on the loop's ribbon count as on the ground at any angle, and a vehicle on it follows the ribbon's line (it is steered along it and held to it, as a car follows a track's curve); everywhere else every vehicle drives exactly as before (`ArcadeVehicle`). Resets inside the park put you on its floor at the nearest clear spot, facing the middle (`VehicleRespawn`).

The park is 40,700 triangles in 6 meshes and 160 colliders, built in 55–135 ms at load.

## Previous delivery — Hwy 92 follow-ups (invisible bump, sidewalks across driveways), trees standing in two Free Roam roads, hidden police still miss one direction, the moon sets in front of the mountains — target 0.105.0-review1 — DELIVERED as 0.105.0-review1 (build 105000), awaiting Dan's review — REVIEW PENDING (Dan will check later; started 0.106 first, 2026-10-10 23:57)

Dan's report `2026-10-10_19-35-11-659_00341f` (0.104.0-review1, Free Roam, mower; ZIP in `C:\Users\danmo\AppData\LocalLow\DefaultCompany\Racer\DebugReports`). Read its four screenshots first. Dan (2026-10-10, 19:50): "cop still seemed to ignore me one way, but got me going the other way. Also, I saw the moon set in front of a mountain instead of disappearing behind it."

Standing rules apply, in particular: world changes only where Dan pointed; checks in the built player with the controller; work in the background (batchmode, windowed minimized test players); never write Dan's real save; signs one-sided and depth-tested; player-facing text American English.

### Part A — Invisible bump on Hwy 92 (BUG-001)

Dan: "I don't see it but there is a bump right here." `FreeRoamWorld`, X=250.33 Y=9.16 Z=557.08, heading 60°, on Hwy 92 by the lane arrows.

1. Find what the mower hits there (a collider above the road surface, a seam between road pieces, a paint or curb piece with a collider, a height step). This is new with 0.104 unless shown otherwise; say what it was and which change added it.
2. Remove the cause. Then sweep the whole refurbished Hwy 92 stretch, in every scene that shows it, for the same kind of thing (any collider other than the road itself that pokes above the drivable surface, any height step over 1 cm across a lane) and fix all of them. Lane paint has no collider.
- **Check:** drive the mower and Dan's usual car over that exact spot in both directions in the built player, with no jolt (report the vertical speed change); list every other spot the sweep found and fixed.

### Part B — Sidewalks run across the driveways (BUG-002)

Dan: "sidewalks shouldn't cross driveways." `FreeRoamWorld`, X=-236.68 Y=9.07 Z=542.31, heading 198°: the concrete sidewalk band runs straight across the store entrance.

1. At every driveway and entrance along the refurbished stretch (all scenes that show it), the driveway pavement runs unbroken from the road edge into the lot; the sidewalk stops on each side of it. Flush, with no lip or bump at either edge.
- **Check:** the screenshot spot, before and after, from Dan's viewpoint; a top-down shot of three entrances; drive in and out of one at speed with no jolt.

### Part C — Trees standing in two Free Roam roads (BUG-003, BUG-004)

1. BUG-003, "tree in road": `FreeRoamWorld`, X=-578.59 Y=11.52 Z=-273.13, heading 310°, on the dirt road at 117 mph.
2. BUG-004, "trees in path": `FreeRoamWorld`, X=-303.76 Y=25.81 Z=-524.46, heading 308°, on the dirt trail.
3. On each of these two roads/trails (the whole length of each, not only the screenshot spot), move every tree whose trunk or collider is inside the drivable width (+1 m) to beside the path, keeping the woods looking as full. Do the same in any race scene that shows these same two paths.
4. Do not touch trees anywhere else. If you notice other trees standing in a drivable road or trail elsewhere, list them with positions for Dan; do not move them.
- **Check:** driver's-view shots at both spots, before and after; drive both paths end to end in the built player with no tree hit.

### Part D — Hidden police still miss you one way (Free Roam)

0.100 removed the facing cone, but Dan still got ignored passing one way and caught passing the other. Find the real cause in Dan's play, don't assume it. Dan (20:11): "I had been driving more than a minute and hadn't seen cops yet" — so no chase had happened yet and the 60 s start quiet had passed; the quiet timers are unlikely, look hardest at the view ray, on-road and the limit. Candidates to rule in or out, each with evidence:
- the quiet timers (`QuietStart` 60 s after Free Roam starts, `QuietAfter` 180 s after a chase ends): turning round right after a chase lands inside the 3-minute quiet;
- the line-of-sight ray blocked on one side (a sign, tree, fence, a new 0.104 building or the cop's own surroundings);
- the speed limit (`SpeedPatrol.LimitAt`) or `onRoad` differing by direction or lane (e.g. the far lane counting as off the road);
- the spot spacing against the 0.2 s check at Dan's speeds (117 mph seen in this report).

1. Fix what is found so that **passing a hidden cop in either direction, in either lane, while over limit + 10 mph, trips the chase every time** the cop has a clear view. If a quiet timer is the cause, keep a quiet period only right after a chase ends, cut it to 30 s, and show nothing confusing (no silent ignore: if a hidden cop saw you during the quiet period, the radio says nothing and nothing happens, which is fine, but say in the delivery notes how long it is).
2. Add to the debug report (the on-screen debug box and `bugs.json`) a short hidden-police line: the nearest active hidden cop's distance, Dan's speed vs. limit + 10, on-road yes/no, clear view yes/no, quiet time remaining. So the next report shows why a pass didn't trip.

### Part E — The moon sets in front of the mountains

1. The moon (and the sun, if it has the same problem) must always be behind everything in the world: terrain, mountains, trees, buildings. It sets *behind* the ridge and disappears, never drawn over it. Draw it as part of the sky (at the far plane / in the sky pass, depth-tested against the world), not as an object at a distance closer than the mountains or drawn on top.

The **0.96 crack repair** (`f9bd8767`, the "-conformed" meshes) had inserted 78 vertices on the highway's two edges at the *terrain's* heights (up to 6.6 cm off the straight edge); each highway triangle spans the full 16.4 m width, so every dent tilted the whole surface. **Not new with 0.104** (0.104 changed no scene or collider; its roadside paint has no collider). **Sweep of the whole stretch (x −800…800), all nine scenes, every 5 cm along 33 lines down the road and every 1 m across it** (`Docs/Report105/Lists/A-sweep-before-*`): no collider other than the road on the drivable width; **two more steps in every scene**: the CR113 seam ribbons **end 6.1–6.3 cm above the road they meet** (8.5–8.7 cm in Free Roam) at **x −505.7** and **x 160.9** (from the CR113 era); and in **Street Loop Reverse** the flush west pavement apron's **west edge stood 14.5 cm above the road at x −508.6** (east edge 1.7 cm at x −491.7). **Fix** ([Report105Road.cs](Tools/Report105/Report105Road.cs); [8 scenes](Docs/Report105/Lists/A-road-fix-8-scenes.txt), [Free Roam](Docs/Report105/Lists/A-road-fix-FreeRoamWorld.txt)): Free Roam's inserted edge vertices put back on their straight edge, with the 156 terrain vertices they were copied from (the edge stays closed); the last 8 m of each seam end eased down onto the road (5 mm above it, measured from the skewed end edge); the apron's first 9 m and last 6 m eased down the same way; the lane paint lying on those spots reseated vertex by vertex (20–35 pieces per scene). New mesh copies in `Assets/Scenery/Report105/`. **After: no step over 1 cm anywhere on the stretch in any of the nine scenes** (`A-sweep-after-*`). **Check (built player, virtual controller, 58 mph, both directions):** at Dan's spot the body's vertical speed changes at most **0.018 / 0.023 m/s** between physics steps (mower east / west) and **0.015 / 0.017 m/s** (Street Classic); over the eased seam ends at most 0.056 m/s ([list](Docs/Report105/Player/bump.txt)); before, the surface itself rippled 2–4 cm every 2 m ([profile](Docs/Report105/Lists/A-profile-before-FreeRoamWorld.txt)).

Rivals still take it.

## Previous delivery — Cabin Jump: the step where the run-up boards meet the roof deck still catches — target 0.103.0-review1 — DELIVERED as 0.103.0-review1 (build 103000) — REVIEWED BY DAN (step fixed; the hop off the run-up is left as is — do not touch the Cabin Jump again unless Dan asks)

- **Authorized by Dan (2026-10-10, 14:15).** Written by Claude (chat) from his play of 0.102.0-review1, debug session `2026-10-10_14-13-22-504_106128` (one report). Dan: "The issue on the ramp is still not fixed. I hit something when it transitions to the roof and it slows me. Happened all 3 times I tried it." Report BUG-001: "it is still catching here on nearly every run."
- **Starting point:** main at the "Record 0.102 delivery" commit. This TODO edit is uncommitted and belongs in the safety checkpoint.
- Runs unattended; stop only for a real external blocker (rule 7). **Scope is exactly Part A.** World changes only at this spot. **Every check in the built Windows player.**

### Part A — Make the run-up boards meet the roof deck with no edge

**Where:** `DansBackyardForward`, Abandoned Cabin Jump branch at 33 m, (213.01, 73.90, 79.69), heading 80°, Turf Rocket, lap 3. Dan's screenshot shows the vehicle on the **lengthwise run-up boards**, and directly ahead the **cross-planked roof / ramp deck** starts with its front edge visibly standing up above the boards: a step across the whole width.

**Why 0.102 missed it:** 0.102 Part E fixed the trail-to-boards foot (the shoulders, s 24.5–35.3) and measured speed "on the transition" at s 23–34 with a scripted driver. The catch Dan hits is the next joint: **boards to roof deck**. The 0.102 numbers do not cover it, and Dan hits it "on nearly every run".

1. **Measure the joint first:** sample the drivable surface (the colliders the wheels touch, not the visible mesh) every 2 cm along the centre line and at ±0.5, ±1.0 and ±1.5 m across, from 2 m before to 2 m after where the boards meet the roof deck. Report every step, gap or reversed slope: the deck's front edge above the boards, a gap between them, two colliders overlapping with an exposed edge, a box collider's corner. Do the same for the visible meshes and say whether they match the colliders.
2. **Fix it at the source:** the boards and the roof deck form one continuous surface at the joint: no step (under 1 cm), no gap, no exposed collider edge or corner the wheels can strike. Preferably one continuous collider across the joint (or the boards' collider carried a short way under and up onto the deck as a smooth wedge). Make the visible deck edge match (the deck's front plank flush with the boards, or a short tapered lip). Do not change the run-up's line or height before the joint, the roof's angle, the lip, the launch or the brush.
3. Same joint in `FreeRoamWorld` (identical run-up and roof since 0.101): same fix.
4. **Check like Dan plays, in the built player:** drive it yourself through the real input path with the Turf Rocket and the Street Classic, five runs each from the main road at racing speed, on the centre, 0.5 m left and 0.5 m right. Log speed every 0.05 s from 3 m before the joint to the lip. **Pass:** no speed drop over 0.3 m/s at the joint in any of the 30 runs (report the worst), and every run takes off clean. Report the joint's before / after surface samples, a short frame sequence of one run across the joint, and a shot from Dan's position.

### Verification

Light except Part A's checks. Compile, launch, release steps. Results as a short list, with "for Dan to check".

The wheels' surface was smooth; the vehicle's body box caught that unjoined edge. **Visible meshes:** the roof planks were level boxes on the 0.32 roof, **each front edge 8.5–9 cm above the roof** (the step in Dan's screenshot); the visible surface did not match the colliders ([before](Docs/Report103/Lists/joint-before.txt)).
